     BF5E BFFC     
4859               cv224
4860               	; 		' HE IS STANDING ON A STEP, so he moves at the STEP's rate: 2 px
4861               	; 		' across and 1 up PER PASS -- the staircase's own slope, and exactly
4862               	; 		' what one phase of esc_tick moves the pattern by.
4863               	; 		'
4864               	; 		' NOT `fdv`, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT. Everything else in this loop
4865               	; 		' is paced by the frame delta, and pacing him that way too made him
4866               	; 		' outrun the steps the moment a pass cost more than one frame: his
4867               	; 		' feet started planted on a tread and ended floating above it.
4868               	; 		' Pacing the ANIMATION by fdv instead is worse -- four phases cannot
4869               	; 		' be stepped by 2 or 3 without aliasing, and the staircase runs
4870               	; 		' backwards (see esc_tick). The only arrangement that cannot drift
4871               	; 		' is the one where both are driven by the same fixed step, so the
4872               	; 		' rider is clocked by the pass, like the steps he is standing on.
4873               	; 		'
4874               	; 		' THE FIRST FEW PASSES ARE THE STEP ON. Walking on, his feet are on
4875               	; 		' the floor and the bottom tread is 4 to 7 px above them, so he
4876               	; 		' climbs at 2 px a pass until he catches it (the tread is rising at
4877               	; 		' 1, so the gap closes at 1 a pass) and then rides with it. Jumping
4878               	; 		' on he lands ON a tread, so eson is 0 and this never runs.
4879               	; 		IF esy < eson THEN esy = esy + 1
4880 BF60 D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESY,r0
     BF62 20F9     
4881 BF64 9800  34 	cb r0,@cvb_ESON
     BF66 20BE

5435               	;
5436               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------------ escalator
5437               	; 	' ONE working escalator per floor, at that floor's alternating end --
5438               	; 	' west, east, west. An escalator drawn where none works would be a step
5439               	; 	' the player runs to and nothing happens.
5440               	; 	' ESCALATORS RUN BOTH WAYS. Stand at the foot and you are carried up;
5441               	; 	' stand at the head of the flight that arrives from the floor below and
5442               	; 	' you are carried down. It is one staircase used from either end, not two
5443               	; 	' -- which is also why the down zone on a floor is the UP zone of the
5444               	; 	' floor beneath it, offset by one band.
5445               	; 	' UP ONLY. Riding down was built at one point and has been taken out
5446               	; 	' again at the reviewer's direction -- these only ever climb.
5447               	; 	'
5448               	; 	' Losing it also removed the re-board lock, which existed solely because
5449               	; 	' stepping off at the head put you inside the zone that rode the same
5450               	; 	' flight back down. With no down ride there is no such zone: a floor's
5451               	; 	' escalator is at the opposite end from the one below it, so arriving at
5452               	; 	' the top of one never lands you on the foot of another.
5453               	; try_esc:
5454               cvb_TRY_ESC

6502               	;
6503               	; 	' THE QUARTER-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR. hx is one unsigned byte per screen (2),
6504               	; 	' so there is nowhere to keep a fraction -- the fraction lives here and
6505               	; 	' is spent as whole pixels. hsp4 = 6 walks 1,2,1,2 (1.5 px/frame); 7
6506               	; 	' walks 1,2,2,2 over four frames (1.75); 8 is a flat 2. Two IFs, not a
6507               	; 	' loop and not a divide: hacc is under 4 on entry and hsp4 is at most 8,
6508               	; 	' so it can never need a third. `%` and `/` both compile to a real
6509               	; 	' TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and this runs every frame.
6510               	; 	'
6511               	; 	' 7, NOT 6, AND THE REASON IS MEASURED. Tracked off the reference video
6512               	; 	' (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen widths a second and
6513               	; 	' its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At hsp4 = 6 ours was 2.67, and the
6514               	; 	' crook visibly strolled. 7 brings it to 2.29. 8 would match the original
6515               	; 	' exactly and cannot be used: Kelly's route here is 1.95x Harry's, so a
6516               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves the on-foot chase 0.2 s of slack, which is no
6517               	; 	' chase at all. checkchase.py holds the line.
6518               	; 	hacc = hacc + hsp4

6807 CDF6 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
6808 CDF8 0450  20 	b *r0
6809               	;
6810               	; 	' ======================================================================
6811               	; 	' COLLISIONS -- pure arithmetic against RAM, no VDP reads
6812               	; 	' ======================================================================
6813               	; 	' Kelly's box is [kfh, kfh + kh) above the slab; an obstacle's is
6814               	; 	' [ohb, oht). The ball's inset hitbox is what makes 8..10 avoidable both
6815               	; 	' ways -- without it there is a height at which the ball can be neither
6816               	; 	' jumped nor ducked, and an unavoidable hazard is not difficulty.
6817               	; 	' ONE HIT PER CONTACT, WITH A REFRACTORY PERIOD.
6818               	; 	'
6819               	; 	' The overlap test is true on every frame the player is touching
6820               	; 	' something, so with no latch at all one clumsy cart charges nine seconds
6821               	; 	' several times over -- and what the player sees is the clock jumping by
6822               	; 	' 27, from which the only available conclusion is that the penalty is
6823               	; 	' broken.
6824               	; 	'
6825               	; 	' A latch that clears on the first non-overlapping frame is still not
6826               	; 	' enough, and the case that breaks it is the BOUNCING BALL: it rises off
6827               	; 	' the player between bounces, which is a genuine loss of contact, so a
6828               	; 	' player standing still under one is taxed on every single bounce while
6829               	; 	' being given no opportunity to do anything about it. Technically two
6830               	; 	' collisions; in play, one situation.
6831               	; 	'
6832               	; 	' So obht() is a countdown, not a flag. A hit sets it to HITREF; it ticks
6833               	; 	' down only while the player is CLEAR, and the same obstacle cannot charge
6834               	; 	' again until it reaches zero. Contact holds the count up, so parking
6835               	; 	' inside something never earns a second penalty either.
6836               	; coll_obst:
6837               cvb_COLL_OBST
6838               	; 	IF klst = ST_ESC THEN RETURN		' riding is invincible, per the

7381               	; 	' KELLY IS SPRITES 0, 1 AND 2 and nothing else ever is -- the VDP drops
7382               	; 	' the highest-numbered sprites on an over-full scanline, so the lowest
7383               	; 	' slots are the ones that can never disappear, and the player is the one
7384               	; 	' thing that must never disappear.
7385               	; 	'
7386               	; 	' THE BAND SPRITES SHARE A y ON PURPOSE. Slots 0 and 1 both sit at ky and
7387               	; 	' between them cover rows 0-15; slot 2 sits at ky+16. Boxes 0/1 and box 2
7388               	; 	' never share a scanline, so an actor costs at most TWO boxes on any line
7389               	; 	' -- and the VDP counts boxes, not pixels, so an empty overlap would have
7390               	; 	' cost just as much as a full one. Two actors meeting is four, exactly
7391               	; 	' the per-line limit, which is why obstacles are suppressed on Harry's
7392               	; 	' floor rather than merely being a kindness.

7507               	; 		' FOUR RUN FRAMES FROM TWO BITS of the animation counter, and no
7508               	; 		' divide -- `/` compiles to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and
7509               	; 		' this is per-frame code. Two adjacent bits give 0,4,8,12.
7510               	; 		'
7511               	; 		' KELLY USES BITS 2 AND 3 AND HARRY USES 3 AND 4, because a stride
7512               	; 		' has to cover about a stride's worth of ground or the figure
7513               	; 		' skates. Kelly runs 4 px a frame, so four frames a pose is 16 px;
7514               	; 		' Harry runs 1.75, so eight frames a pose is 14. Matching the two
7515               	; 		' rates to the two speeds is what keeps both looking like running.

8102 D902 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
8103 D904 0450  20 	b *r0
8104               	;
8105               	; 	' ======================================================================
8106               	; 	' THE SCANNER
8107               	; 	' ======================================================================
8108               	; 	' Rows 21-23, 16 chars wide, centred: 48 characters whose PATTERNS are
8109               	; 	' poked directly, so a moving dot costs two writes and no name-table
8110               	; 	' traffic. With the flip (DESIGN.md 2a) this is how you know where Harry
8111               	; 	' is at all -- he can be one pixel off-screen and completely invisible.
8112               	; 	'
8113               	; 	' Rows 21-23 all sit in the THIRD screen third, so every scanner pattern
8114               	; 	' is at base 4096 and no third-selection arithmetic is needed.
8115               	; 	'
8116               	; 	' BOTH DOTS ARE WHITE and Kelly's BLINKS. The manual wants Kelly black
8117               	; 	' and Harry white, but this is the TMS9918's two-colours-per-8x1-cell
8118               	; 	' mode: a black dot and a white dot on the same pixel row of the same
8119               	; 	' character cannot both exist. Blinking distinguishes them with one
8120               	; 	' colour, which is what RallyX does with its player dot.
8121               	; 	' THE STEPS MOVE, AND EVERY ESCALATOR ON SCREEN MOVES WITH THEM. A flight
8122               	; 	' is twelve characters -- six per direction, sliced out of a rendered
8123               	; 	' 64x40 bitmap by genart.py -- so rewriting those twelve PATTERNS animates
8124               	; 	' the lot: 96 bytes, no name-table traffic, and the same cost whether one
8125               	; 	' flight is visible or ten. The phases slide the cells ALONG the slope, so
8126               	; 	' the steps travel up the flight rather than drifting sideways.
8127               	; esc_tick:
8128               cvb_ESC_TICK
8129               	; 	' ONLY SCREENS 0 AND 7 CARRY A FLIGHT, so six screens in eight skip this
8130               	; 	' entirely -- which is what makes rewriting the cells every frame
8131               	; 	' affordable. Nested, not `klsc > 0 AND klsc < 7`: the 9900 backend
8132               	; 	' miscompiles a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).
8133               	; 	IF klsc > 0 THEN
8134 D906 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     D908 2149     
8135 D90A 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     D90C 0100     
8136 D90E 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
8137 D910 1402  14 	jhe cv611
8138 D912 0460  28 	b @cv610
     D914 D92A     
8139               cv611
8140               	; 		IF klsc < 7 THEN RETURN
8141 D916 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     D918 2149     
8142 D91A 0201  20 	li r1,1792   ; 7*256
     D91C 0700     
8143 D91E 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
8144 D920 1A02  14 	jl cv613
8145 D922 0460  28 	b @cv612
     D924 D92A     
8146               cv613
8147 D926 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
8148 D928 0450  20 	b *r0
8149               cv612
8150               	; 	END IF
8151               cv610
8152               	; 	' ONE PHASE PER PASS, AND NEVER MORE. There are four phases covering an
8153               	; 	' 8 px period, so advancing by the frame delta ALIASES: at fdv 2 the
8154               	; 	' pattern flips between two positions half a period apart and the
8155               	; 	' direction stops being readable, and at fdv 3 the sequence runs 0,3,2,1
8156               	; 	' -- the staircase visibly runs BACKWARDS, carrying its steps down while
8157               	; 	' the rider goes up. That is the wagon-wheel effect, and no amount of
8158               	; 	' phase arithmetic fixes it: a 4-phase cycle can only be stepped by 1.
8159               	; 	'
8160               	; 	' So the ANIMATION is the clock here, and the rider is paced from it --
8161               	; 	' see move_kelly, which advances him by the same fixed 2-and-1 per pass
8162               	; 	' rather than by fdv. Locking the two together is the only way they
8163               	; 	' cannot drift, and it is what "the player moves at the rate the steps
8164               	; 	' carry him" actually means. The cost is that a ride takes 36 passes
8165               	; 	' rather than 36 frames, so it slows down when the loop does -- which is
8166               	; 	' the honest behaviour for a machine that is carrying you.
8167               	; 	escp = escp + 1
8168 D92A D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESCP,r0
     D92C 20AF     
8169 D92E 0220  22 	ai r0,256	; 1
     D930 0100     
8170 D932 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ESCP
     D934 20AF

     D938 0400

     D940 D948

8383 DAF4 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
8384 DAF6 0450  20 	b *r0
8385               	;
8386               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------- the scanner's furniture
8387               	; 	' Without this the scanner is two dots in a void: you can see THAT Harry is
8388               	; 	' somewhere, but not what he is near, which is the one thing you actually
8389               	; 	' need when he is off-screen. Floor lines say which level, the slashes say
8390               	; 	' where that floor's escalator is, and the bar says where the elevator is --
8391               	; 	' exactly the three things the manual lists.
8392               	; 	'
8393               	; 	' FURNITURE AND DOTS ARE SEGREGATED BY PIXEL ROW ON PURPOSE, and a level
8394               	; 	' band is now FOUR rows, one per colour, because a pixel row carries
8395               	; 	' exactly one colour here:
8396               	; 	'
8397               	; 	'   +0  GREY    escalator head, and the elevator car
8398               	; 	'   +1  BLACK   Kelly -- and the escalator's FOOT, which the manual
8399               	; 	'               draws black anyway, so the diagonal spans both rows
8400               	; 	'   +2  WHITE   Harry
8401               	; 	'   +3  YELLOW  the floor line
8402               	; 	'
8403               	; 	' Four is the floor: the Kop, the crook, the furniture and the floor
8404               	; 	' lines are four different colours and none of them can share. Shrinking
8405               	; 	' from six bought eight empty pixel rows, four above the instrument and
8406               	; 	' four below, so it no longer butts against the shop floor above it or
8407               	; 	' the bottom of the screen.
8408               	; 	'
8409               	; 	' The dots are erased by ANDing their bits out, so anything sharing a row
8410               	; 	' with one is rubbed away wherever an actor has passed -- which is why
8411               	; 	' scan_escs and scan_elev redraw every tick rather than once.
8412               	; scan_furn:
8413               cvb_SCAN_FURN
8414               	; 	FOR fl = 0 TO 3

8416 DAFA D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_FL

9793               	; 	GOTO main
9794 E634 0460  28 	b @cvb_MAIN
     E636 AA16     
9795               	;
9796               	; 	' REPEATED ADDITION, not a multiply, and deliberately so. Two separate
9797               	; 	' hazards meet on this one line: `tsec * bmul.` is invalid because the `.`
9798               	; 	' suffix marks a 16-bit CONSTANT and both of these are variables, and a
9799               	; 	' plain `tsec * bmul` would be an 8-bit product -- a 50-second capture in
9800               	; 	' the x300 band pays 15,000, which is six times past a byte. Multiplying
9801               	; 	' properly would then land on the MPY/r0 hazard, where a 16-bit var read
9802               	; 	' straight after being multiplied returns the product's HIGH word.
9803               	; 	'
9804               	; 	' At most 50 adds, once per capture. Nothing about this is a hot path.
9805               	; 	'
9806               	; 	' The guard matters: a COMPUTED `FOR 1 TO 0` still runs its body once in
9807               	; 	' CVBasic, so catching Harry on the very last tick would pay a full
9808               	; 	' multiplier instead of nothing.
9809               	; calc_bonus:
9810               cvb_CALC_BONUS
9811               	; 	#bonus = 0
9812 E638 04E0  34 	clr @cvb__BONUS
     E63A 20D4     
9813               	; 	IF tsec > 0 THEN
9814 E63C D020  34 	movb @cvb_TSEC,r0
     E63E 20BD     
9815 E640 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     E642 0100     
9816 E644 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
9817 E646 1402  14 	jhe cv733
9818 E648 0460  28 	b @cv732
     E64A E680     
9819               cv733
9820               	; 		FOR bi = 1 TO tsec
9821 E64C 0200  20 	li r0,256   ; 1*256
     E64E 0100     
9822 E650 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_BI
     E652 2146     
9823               cv734
9824               	; 			#bonus = #bonus + bmul
9825 E654 D020  34 	movb @cvb_BMUL,r0
     E656 2083     
9826 E658 0980  32 	srl r0,8
9827 E65A C040  18 	mov r0,r1
9828 E65C C020  34 	mov @cvb__BONUS,r0
     E65E 20D4     
9829 E660 A001  18 	a r1,r0
9830 E662 C800  38 	mov r0,@cvb__BONUS
     E664 20D4     
9831               	; 		NEXT bi
9832 E666 D020  34 	movb @cvb_BI,r0
     E668 2146     
9833 E66A 0220  22 	ai r0,256	; 1
     E66C 0100     
9834 E66E D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_BI
     E670 2146     
9835 E672 D020  34 	movb @cvb_TSEC,r0
     E674 20BD     
9836 E676 9800  34 	cb r0,@cvb_BI
     E678 2146     
9837 E67A 1A02  14 	jl cv735
9838 E67C 0460  28 	b @cv734
     E67E E654

10047               	; 	' SOUND
10048               	; 	' ======================================================================
10049               	; 	' Every effect gets an explicit note-off. SOUND latches -- with no
10050               	; 	' `SOUND ch,f,0` the last tone sustains for ever. And the second argument
10051               	; 	' is a 10-BIT DIVISOR, max 1023: SMALLER IS HIGHER, so a rising sweep is
10052               	; 	' written as a falling number and anything over 1023 is silently masked
10053               	; 	' to an unrelated pitch.

10401 EB54 0450  20 	b *r0
10402               	;
10403               	; 	' The font stays in bank 0: it is small, and keeping one readable thing
10404               	; 	' unbanked makes a bank-selection mistake obvious (text survives, art does
10405               	; 	' not) instead of producing a uniformly blank screen.
10406               	; 	INCLUDE "font.bas"
10407               	; 	' Keystone Kapers font -- GENERATED by assets/genfont.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
10408               	; 	'
10409               	; 	' Characters 32-90 (59 of them), 8 bytes each = 472 bytes. Williams' cabinet face:
10410               	; 	' heavy, squared, flat terminals, no serifs -- drawn 5x7 inside the 8x8 cell so a
10411               	; 	' blank column at the right and a blank row at the bottom keep it legible.
10412               	; 	'
10413               	; 	' Loaded with DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits. The run must be CONTIGUOUS, so slots
10414               	; 	' with no glyph emit blanks rather than being skipped -- a gap would shift every
10415               	; 	' letter after it.
10416               	;
10417               	; font_bits:
10418               cvb_FONT_BITS
10419               	; 	DATA BYTE $00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00	' space

10538               	; 	' EVERYTHING BELOW THIS LINE IS ASSEMBLED INTO BANK 1, so the INCLUDE order
10539               	; 	' is load-bearing and nothing but data may follow it. Putting the directive
10540               	; 	' above font.bas would sweep the font in too.
10541               	; 	#if TI994A
10542               	; 	BANK 1
10543      12D0     BANK_0_FREE:	EQU >fffe-$

10550               	; 	INCLUDE "art.bas"
10551               	; 	' Keystone Kapers art -- GENERATED by assets/genart.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
10552               	; 	'
10553               	; 	' EVERY SPRITE IS 16x16: sprite size and magnification are GLOBAL on the
10554               	; 	' TMS9918, so small objects are drawn small INSIDE a 16x16 box rather than
10555               	; 	' being small sprites.
10556               	; 	'
10557               	; 	' EVERY 8-PX OBJECT SITS IN ROWS 8-15 OF ITS BOX, ducked Kelly included, so
10558               	; 	' one rule places them all: bottom edge = y + 16, and a thing resting on the
10559               	; 	' floor is at y = FLOORY - 16.
10560               	; 	'
10561               	; 	' Sprite bytes are QUADRANT-ORDERED: 16 rows of the left half, then 16 of
10562               	; 	' the right. Every block is an EVEN number of bytes -- an odd DATA BYTE run
10563               	; 	' silently misaligns every word table after it (CLAUDE.md 3A).
10564               	;
10565               	; spr_kelly:	' Kelly: RIGHT hat/face/tunic/tunic-arm-up/duck-hat/duck-face, then the same LEFT (+24), then the FOUR shared run frames. Patterns 0..60
10566               cvb_SPR_KELLY

11232               	;
11233               	; 	' -------------------------------------------------- scanner canvas
11234               	; 	' Chars 160-207: 16 cols x 3 rows over screen rows 21-23. Blank to start;
11235               	; 	' dots are plotted by VPOKEing the PATTERN table (rows 21-23 sit in the
11236               	; 	' third screen third, so every scanner pattern is at base 4096).
11237               	;
11238               	; 	' --------------------------------------------------- font colours
11239               	; 	' Chars 32-90, EIGHT bytes each. Without this the font keeps whatever
11240               	; 	' CVBasic left in the colour table, which on a green store made the HUD
11241               	; 	' unreadable.
11242               	;
11243               	; scan_col3:	' one 8-byte block per canvas row: 4 px of GREY margin, then furniture grey / Kelly black / Harry white / floor line yellow per 4 px level, on dark green
11244               cvb_SCAN_COL3

11312               	; 	'   155 ENDWALL
11313               	; 	'   156 SCANBK
11314               	; 	INCLUDE "store.bas"
11315               	; 	' Keystone Kapers store -- GENERATED by assets/genstore.py. Do not edit.
11316               	; 	'
11317               	; 	' EIGHT band templates of 5 rows x 32 cols (160 bytes each) plus a 32-byte
11318               	; 	' index over (level, screen). The whole 8-screen, 4-level building is about
11319               	; 	' 1.3 KB instead of the 5,120 bytes it would cost to store literally.
11320               	; 	'
11321               	; 	' All eight templates are ONE contiguous block under ONE label, so a band
11322               	; 	' blit is
11323               	; 	'     SCREEN stor_tpl, tpl*160, toprow*32, 32, 5, 32
11324               	; 	' with no label ladder -- SCREEN needs a literal label and a label cannot be
11325               	; 	' chosen by a variable, which is why RallyX needed a 4-way IF over its maze
11326               	; 	' tables. One block sidesteps it.
11327               	; 	'
11328               	; 	' 160 is EVEN, so this cannot leave the location counter odd and misalign
11329               	; 	' the word tables defined after it.
11330               	; 	'
11331               	; 	' template ids:
11332               	; 	'   0 T_AISLE_A
11333               	; 	'   1 T_AISLE_B
11334               	; 	'   2 T_ESC_W
11335               	; 	'   3 T_ESC_E
11336               	; 	'   4 T_ELEV
11337               	; 	'   5 T_ROOF
11338               	; 	'   6 T_ROOF_W
11339               	; 	'   7 T_ROOF_E
11340               	; 	'   8 T_END_W
11341               	; 	'   9 T_END_E
11342               	;
11343               	; stor_tpl:	' 10 templates x 160 bytes
11344               cvb_STOR_TPL
11345               	; 	DATA BYTE 148,148,148,148,98,148,148,148,148,148,148,98,148,148,148,148
